[26-06-2026 2:38 PM] <l0rd.helmet> What’s happening this morning – chip/component sentiment witnessed a brief rejuvenation on Thurs thanks to the robust Micron results, but the mood soured once again overnight, as investors (as was the case Mon-Wed) turn their back to the sector. What caused the Thurs night/Fri morning pivot?  The single most important headline was the NYT report about how OpenAI might delay its IPO from Q3-4:26 into 2027, a deferral that could slow the pace of infrastructure spending (since the bulk of the capex boom is being funded by capital markets as opposed to op. cash flow). Meanwhile, OpenAI apparently received explicit instructions from Washington (according to The Information) to stagger the release of its newest model (GPT 5.6), underscoring how the White House is becoming increasingly restrictive when it comes to frontier technology. Frontier labs are witnessing pressure from all sides: improved capabilities invite a regulatory backlash while customers are pushing back on cost and because of this, open-source Chinese models are capturing a growing share of token consumption (this last issue was the subject of a recent NYT article: “Chinese A.I. Models Close the Gap With Anthropic and OpenAI”). When it comes to memory/components, the industry is performing extremely well right now, but investors are increasingly nervous that the numbers aren’t sustainable given demand destruction (as hardware OEMs push through price hikes, as was the case w/Apple and Microsoft on Thursday) and new supply (media reports suggest Samsung and SK Hynix will both outline massive spending initiatives on Monday aimed at a slew of products and initiatives, including expanding memory availability). 

(Adam Crisafulli)

[13-07-2026 4:49 AM] <yahyaz> 'Well, the forecast for this weekend looks a lot like it did on Friday morning.

And our conclusion is the same: We cannot possibly not own an OTM put when there's a couple percent chance of some seriously crashy action. We're already filled in the 7000-strike across a couple tenors (centered on 1-month). Tangentially, part of the idea of buying at a very round-number strike is that if things do fall apart, you're much more likely to find a real, two-sided market at the round numbers. Even when the quoting machines are turned off, some folks will be trading that strike by hand. Slow, small-batch, artisanal trading.
 
So, just a consideration. For future conditional liquidity.'

[17-07-2026 1:35 AM] <attackofthejax> A narrowing us- Japan yield differential  is supportive for the yen? Groundbreaking
[] <attackofthejax> They will have to defend eventually
[17-07-2026 2:07 AM] <attackofthejax> They will try to wait for the war to end and oil to fall (as major importers of oil) to give them a better chance. They hold a lot of us debt as firepower but there’s the catch 22 of liquidating their reserves too hastily —> if they pressure t markets us yields rise which hurts the yen. Unlikely as treasury  markets are (obviously) very deeply liquid Japan holds more than 3% of outstanding treasuries, but their entire supply is less than a days worth of treasury volume’s traded. There would still be a short term shock, but I think treasury markets are probably already trying to price in Japan turning into net sellers of treasuries. Although maybe not… the Japanese bond sell of in January (19bps rise in Japan 10y bond yield in 2 days) spooked us treasuries pretty bad. That was short lived, but will be peanuts compared to a big yen defense.  

They’ll start with verbal intervention, but that is maybe all it will take to start an unwind of levered yen-funded positions. 

I think one think that mirrors 2024 is short index vol positioning. The short index vol long single stock vol rv trade is crowded.
[] <attackofthejax> I think claiming that the us equity markets (and this massive rally)  is substantially propped up by yen- funded us equity purchases is unfounded and a big stretch. Although  how much systemic risk there is in the yen carry is unknown, you can look at CFTC data, but that won’t show you exposure in opaque otc markets (swaps, forwards, options) where the vast majority of leverage lies.
[17-07-2026 2:54 AM] <attackofthejax> They will have to defend eventually, while Japanese equities have benefited from a weaker yen the average person in Japan is not exposed to equities the same way Americans are through their 401ks. (Although they have exposure through their pensions,., only the retirees are feeling those benefits in the form of a slightly more valuable monthly check.) I think Japanese pension funds are about 25% Japanese equities. 
the average Japanese person is not feeling their wealth increasing as their purchasing power decreases. My friends over there are tired of watching foreigners earning in dollars and euros gawk at 711s and swipe their credit cards with care free jubilance!

Maybe that’s a negative catalyst for the war ending and oil prices falling/ stabilizing. Japan having a stronger position to mount a more comprehensive yen defense

[20-07-2026 5:35 AM] <attackofthejax> Long index gamma and Volga I assume? 
Usually there’s a trade off between the two unless you’re running a layered options book across multiple expirations. So maybe near dated closer to spot strangle with longer tenor further otm strangle (long the wings.) the hope there is to monetize spot convexity while being long vol convexity. 

Or maybe he’s being loose with his terminology. He could be long Vix atm straddles which is strictly technically not long index Volga but can give you the same exposure. That way you can monetize spot convexity in Vix but also give you long index convexity (economically but not  in 1:1 terms.) he would probably technically be short Volga as higher gamma atm straddles usually carry 0 or slightly negative Volga 

That last one is pretty expensive to hedge though.

Maybe he specified because he’s Vega neutral? Also expensive, uncommon and unlikely
[] <attackofthejax> Or maybe he’s trying to sound smart and he’s just long Vix calls! That’s long gamma and *sometimes long index spx Volga in some places. I doubt a professional would use that terminology to describe simply owning vix calls 

Big asterix on sometimes
[] <attackofthejax> Or maybe it’s a term structure trade and he’s short Vega
[] <attackofthejax> Or honestly probably just medium tenor downside wings (edited)
[] <attackofthejax> Long medium dated otm puts basically
[20-07-2026 5:47 AM] <attackofthejax> But he would probably say long the wings… he is probably trying to actively monetize spot convexity or wouldn’t have mentioned it
[] <attackofthejax> If you’re expecting imminent violent acceleration of vol while trying to pay for theta (probably because he’s burned a whole lot of premium long index vol already.) this can be a good trade.
[] <attackofthejax> I mean honestly long gamma short Vega long Volga would work well. So you monetize spot convexity as spot moves around, you’re short vol (locally), but long vol convexity (edited)
[] <attackofthejax> If this short correlation  trade unravels slowly and orderly you’ll get screwed there
[] <attackofthejax> That’s also a possibility for the correlation trade…orderly risk reduction … the slow burn
